# Full finding list — automated review of the book (8 of 25 chunks reviewed, 4 verified)

Each row is one reviewer finding (technical-accuracy lens or editorial lens) with the adversarial verifier's ruling. **Verdict**: `confirmed` (verifier re-read the passage and agrees), `refuted` (kept for transparency — the verifier explains why the text is fine), `duplicate` (same issue as another row), `uncertain`, `verifier-added` (found by the verifier itself), or `book-wide check` (from the eight cross-cutting agents, which were not separately verified). **Sev** is the verifier's severity when it ruled, else the reviewer's. Line numbers are approximate.

| confirmed | medium | 114 | unclear-or-misleading |         auto k = keys[pick(rng)];               // unpredictable key each iter | The timed loop contains an mt19937 draw plus uniform_int_distribution (a multiply/rejection step) and an index load, and consecutive iterations are independent, so the out-of-order core overlaps the RNG and the next lookup with the current one. | confirmed | medium | 199 | technical-inaccuracy | A frequency-independent cross-check: measure in **cycles** (via `rdtscp` or PMU `cycles`, Ch. 17) as well as nanoseconds. If two runs dis… | rdtsc/rdtscp read the invariant TSC, which ticks at a constant nominal rate independent of the core's P-state — it is a wall-clock, not a core-cycle counter. TSC ticks are strictly proportional to nanoseconds, so a TSC measurement can never 'agree in cycles while disagreeing in ns'; only the PMU `cycles` event (CPU_CLK_UNHALTED.THREAD) counts actual core clocks. Ch. 17 (§17.2.1 and its pitfalls list) explicitly ca… | 'A frequency-independent cross-check for core-bound code: measure in core cycles via the PMU cycles event (perf stat -e cycles, or rdpmc) as well as in ns (clock_gettime/rdtscp — the TSC is a constant-rate clock, not a core-cycle counter; Ch. 17). "if x was negative (sign flag)" associates SF with the wrong case. | `; SF set by neg ⇔ -x < 0 ⇔ x > 0: move x (edi) back in; otherwise (x ≤ 0) leave -x` — optionally note that `-INT_MIN` is UB in the C++ source. |

| unverified | medium | 130 | technical-inaccuracy | SQPOLL + IOPOLL + CQ-polling = multiple cores at 100% (kernel poller + your poller + device poller) | There is no separate 'device poller' thread/core.
